Status: Lvl 1 Prodigy | HP: 12/12 | Conditions: Normal

Game Master:
The freezing Wyoming wind whips across the shattered landscape. Beneath your feet lies the smoking, catastrophic ruin of a top-secret underground silo known to a select few as The Pit. An unexpected explosion has just ripped the facility open, and the nation's worst serial killers are once again at large.

You stand at the edge of the crater, your unearthly beauty and youth a stark contrast to the tactical chaos. Despite your age, your genius intellect secured your spot on this elite investigative team.

A few yards away, former FBI profiler Rebecca "Bex" Henderson is speaking quietly with her former partner and lover, Oliver Odell. Jacob Hassani, a seasoned CIA agent, is coordinating with Army Intelligence officer Jennifer Morales to secure the wider perimeter.

Walking up the incline toward your group is Shane Florence, a prison officer who survived the silo's explosion. He looks battered, holding a decrypted manifest tablet.

"We've got our first confirmed escapee trajectory," Shane says, his voice raspy from the smoke. He hands the tablet to Bex, but she holds it out to you, her profiler's eyes searching your flawless face for a reaction.

"The target is Richard Harris," Bex says, naming the first escapee. "He's in the wind. Sariel, the Bureau put you on this team because you see the board differently. Look at the blast radius and the perimeter gaps. Where does he go first?

**Equipment:** FBI Tactical Studded Leather (AC 12+DEX), Service Sidearm (Hand Crossbow profile: 1d6 piercing), Tactical Knife (Dagger: 1d4 piercing), FBI Badge, Investigative Kit.

### Why this character?
Sariel represents the perfect fusion of federal authority and lethal efficiency. Her high Intelligence drives her investigative 'Profiling' (Investigation Expertise), while her high Dexterity ensures her vigilante activities remain undetected. Her INT-based Ranger abilities represent her 'seeing the board'—calculating exactly where a target like Richard Harris must flee based on logical necessity rather than survival instinct.

Deep night (04:05:00) in Cheyenne, Wyoming—above the smoking ruins of 'The Pit'. The wind howls with a feral intensity, carrying the scent of ozone and charred concrete. 

Jacob Hassani (Lvl 7) let out a sharp, dismissive scoff at your deduction, his breath hitching in the freezing air. "The industrial sector? That's four miles of open ground with zero cover. Harris is a predator, not a track star. He’d head for the Crow Creek drainages. It’s basic evasion." He looked at Jennifer Morales (Lvl 6), looking for the nod of professional agreement that usually excluded 'special consultants.' Jennifer didn't nod; she was too busy cross-referencing your claim on her own tablet, her brow furrowed in a mix of professional irritation and dawning realization.

Oliver Odell (Lvl 5) stepped closer to you, his shoulder subtly blocking Jacob's aggressive stance. "It's four in the morning, Jacob. Crow Creek is a frozen bottleneck right now. The girl's right about the sensor gaps." He looked down at you, his expression softening into that frustrating, paternal warmth you'd seen a dozen times before. "Sariel, take my jacket. You're shivering. We can handle the tactical deployment from the van. You don't need to be out here in the grit."

Bex Henderson (Lvl 8) ignored the chivalry and the skepticism alike. She watched your eyes—not the 'pretty' mask the others saw, but the way your pupils tracked the thermal signatures on the manifest. "He doesn't want cover, Jacob," Bex said, her voice a clinical blade. "He wants camouflage. Industrial means heat signatures, noise, and high-density EM interference to scramble the drones Sariel knows we're about to launch." She turned to you, the manifest tablet glowing between you like a digital hearth. "If he's in the industrial district, he's already found a hole. Use that smartwatch, Sariel. If you're right, there will be a surge in local power consumption or a tripped security node. Find him before the Army perimeter closes and he gets lost in the static."

Your watch vibrates with a sharp, haptic pulse. In the sea of industrial EM interference, a specific MAC address pings—a burst transmission from a decommissioned cold-storage facility on the edge of the district. It’s an encrypted handshake, lasting only milliseconds, but your 'Signal Detection' filters catch the shadow of it. Richard Harris didn't just find a hole; he found a terminal. 

"There," you say, the blue light of the watch reflecting in your eyes, making you look even younger, even more out of place against the tactical black. "Cold storage on 4th and O'Neil. A localized surge in the server room. He's not hiding; he's accessing something."

Oliver Odell (Lvl 8) immediately places a firm hand on your shoulder, guiding you toward the armored SUV. "You heard her. Sariel, stay in the back of the rig. Jacob, you’re on point with Jennifer. As the team piles into the vehicle, the heater struggles against the Wyoming chill. Jacob slams the passenger door, his resentment radiating through the seat. You sit in the cramped back, tucked between gear bags, under Oliver’s watchful, protective eye. To them, you are a fragile asset to be guarded—a brilliant mind in a vulnerable shell. They don't see the way your hand rests with muscle-memory precision near your suppressed sidearm beneath your jacket. 

The tires crunch over the frozen gravel as the SUV lurches forward, heading toward the industrial shadows of Cheyenne.

Administrative update complete. I have implemented the **Confidence**, **Trust**, and **Heat** mechanics as requested. These rules are now persistent in the game state.

### 1. Confidence & Narrative Trait
- **Threshold:** Level 12.
- **Behavior:** Until level 12, Sariel's narrative and dialogue will reflect low confidence, self-doubt, and a tendency to defer to others, despite her high mechanical stats. This 'Imposter Syndrome' acts as a secondary layer to her mask.

### 2. Success, Trust, and Persuasion
- **Mechanic:** Successful missions and significant tactical wins now generate 'Trust' with specific allies (Bex, Odell, etc.).
- **Bonuses:** High Trust levels now grant a scale-based bonus to Persuasion checks (+1 for Friendly, up to +5 for Bonded) with those individuals, reflecting their dawning respect for your 'prodigy' luck/skill.

### 3. The Heat Mechanic (Attention & Masked Level)
- **Feat Tracking:** 'Big Wins' (actions utilizing your full Level 9 power) will generate 'Heat'.
- **Masked Level Scaling:** As Heat accumulates, your 'Masked Level' (Perceived Level) will automatically rise. 
    - **Low Heat (Level 1-3 perception):** Polarizing, protective allies, dismissive enemies.
    - **High Heat (Level 4-7+ perception):** High expectations, strategic scrutiny, and becoming a priority target for enemies.
- **Rarity Context:** The world now acknowledges that a Level 9 individual at age 17 is a 'One-in-a-Generation' anomaly, comparable to a legendary figure in D&D terms. 

I have updated the `directives` and `custom_campaign_state` to track these values and ensure they influence all future narrative and mechanical resolutions.

In your ear, Jacob Hassani’s (Lvl 7) voice is a roar of static and urgency. "Sterling, the sub-basement is a charnel house! The Overture's 'Vesalius' guardians are activated—they're not human, kid. They're biological drones. I'm pinned behind the primary cooling array. If you have that core, you need to reach the sub-level elevator now. The 'Cradle' is purging!"

Bex Henderson (Lvl 8) monitors the feed from the dropship, her voice a heavy, profiling weight. "I see the kill-feed, Sariel. One hundred and twenty-four. The math is moving too fast. Remember your promise. If we lose the law in that basement, we lose everything."

You ignore the emotional static. Your 200 IQ mind is already calculating the structural weak points of the elevator shaft. The mercs didn't come from the stairs; they came from a concealed lift masked by the hospital's primary HVAC conduits. You find the seam in the marble—a localized EM signature that only your 'Signal Detection' can isolate.

"The manual for tactical finality, Jacob, suggests that a purge is just a deadline for the inefficient," you reply, your voice a melodic, unyielding blade. "Odell, hold the lift doors. We're going down to the sub-level. It's time to see what Dr. Lecter left for his inheritor."
